[a11y-image-desc] Guard AXImageAnnotator::OnImageAnnotated re-entrancy

Updating layout and AX across all documents inside OnImageAnnotated()
can synchronously run script or frame observers and detach the frame or
deactivate the document, destroying `this`. Require a non-null active AX
context before and after UpdateAXForAllDocuments(), and guard `this`
with a WeakPtr across the update.

AX-Relnotes: n/a.

Original change's description:
> WebNN: Copy compiled CoreML model directly without base::Move
>
> Have the compiler process move CoreML's compiled output directly to
> `$TMPDIR/webnn_compiler_protected/<scoped_dir_A>` without an extra
> `model.mlmodelc` subdirectory. The browser process can then copy
> `<scoped_dir_A>` directly to
> `$TMPDIR/webnn_gpu_protected/<scoped_dir_B>/model.mlmodelc` using
> `base::CopyDirectoryNoFollow`, avoiding `base::Move` (which falls back
> to symlink-following `base::CopyDirectory` on failure) and
> `base::DeletePathRecursively`.
>

Ash: Make read write cards UI own its widget

This changelist is part of an ongoing series to move Ash widgets off
the deprecated NATIVE_WIDGET_OWNS_WIDGET and WIDGET_OWNS_NATIVE_WIDGET
ownership modes and onto CLIENT_OWNS_WIDGET, the mode views recommends
for all widgets. With it, the code that creates a widget owns it and
decides when it is destroyed. This removes a common source of dangling
pointers and window teardown bugs.

The read write cards UI controller now owns the widget holding the
Quick Answers and Mahi cards, and destroys it when its last card is
removed or when its window goes away.

Bug: 338254375

[cbor] Bundle FuzzTest seed corpus on iOS

FUZZ_TESTs in //components/cbor:unit_tests load seed corpus files from
base::DIR_SRC_TEST_DATA_ROOT via fuzztest::ReadFilesFromDirectory.

On iOS, DIR_SRC_TEST_DATA_ROOT resolves to the .app bundle root, so GN
`data` dependencies alone do not make these files available at runtime.
Package reader_fuzzer_corpus using bundle_data_from_filelist so tests do
not fail setup when FuzzTest registration is enabled on iOS.

Generated iOS filelist with build/ios/update_bundle_filelist.py.

Bug: 577661175

Ash: Migrate recent apps browser tests off RunUntilIdle

This changelist is part of an ongoing series to modernize Ash tests by
migrating them away from RunUntilIdle() and toward targeted
synchronization with APIs such as RunUntil(), TestFuture, RunLoop
callbacks, observer waiters, or exact mock-time advancement.

AppListWithRecentAppBrowserTest does not need to drain pending tasks
between activating the profile and installing its test apps. Profile
activation sets the launcher model directly, and showing the launcher
already waits for the bubble after refreshing its suggestions. Remove
the extra setup drain while keeping the existing launcher waits.

Bug: b:506794349
Test: CQ

parent
ab4aedea98586f68da830892055ce8e865da299c [ diff ] 
[iOS][STTS] Show omnibox IPH on first user navigation or tab switch

The Send Tab to Self omnibox IPH was attempted once on the startup tab
when the browser view first became visible. That bubble was often
dismissed right away by startup UI changes, and was also shown when the
entry point only offered sign-in or reauth.

Never show the IPH on the startup tab. Instead, show it on the first
user navigation or tab switch that lands on an eligible page. The IPH
now requires the `kOfferFeature` display reason, while the omnibox entry
point continues accepting any reason.

Fixed: 578508448

[Composebox] Fix caret flashing at text start on mouse refocus

Before:
https://screencast.googleplex.com/cast/NDUyOTk3MDQwNjc1MjI1NnxjYmU4MWJhMi0xMQ

After:
https://screencast.googleplex.com/cast/NDY2OTA4NTIwMjc3NjA2NHxkNTFmZjRiZS03ZQ

On mouse refocus, the focus event fires before the selection moves to
the click point, so the custom caret was drawn at the text start until
click.

A rAF now re-anchors it after the selection is placed and before paint;
the existing synchronous update is kept.

📋 Metadata

1e1042dfcb77179aecc89d8d6b24c11b07f75366

tree
2b074c4d9e48d58a10e31622cd7ff11fd244acbc
parent
4c0861bb60dcec3c51efe2049be39703be1aee39 [ diff ] 
Fix UAF and safe deletion in ChromeOS VpnService configurations

When CreateConfiguration() creates a VPN configuration in Shill, two
async flows run concurrently: Shill's OnConfigurationCreated observer
(via OnGetShillProperties) and the direct CreateShillConfiguration
reply (OnCreateConfigurationSuccess / OnCreateConfigurationFailure).
Previously, CreateConfiguration() bound a raw VpnConfiguration*
pointer into the completion callbacks. If the configuration was
registered via OnGetShillProperties and then destroyed (e.g. by a
concurrent destroyConfig call in VpnProviderApiTest.ComboSuite,
extension unload, or OnConfigurationRemoved) before
OnCreateConfigurationSuccess ran, the callback dereferenced a
dangling VpnConfiguration* pointer in RegisterConfiguration().

Additionally, ~VpnService() destroyed remaining VpnConfiguration
objects in key_to_configuration_map_ without calling
RemoveShillThirdPartyVpnObserver(), leaving dangling observer
pointers in ShillThirdPartyVpnDriverClient.

To fix these issues:
1. Pass extension_id and configuration_name by value to
   OnCreateConfigurationSuccess and OnCreateConfigurationFailure
   instead of binding a raw VpnConfiguration* pointer, and look up
   the configuration via LookupConfiguration() when the callbacks
   run.
2. Unregister ShillThirdPartyVpnObserver in ~VpnConfiguration() so
   observers are always cleaned up on destruction (including
   VpnService shutdown).
3. Use find() with CHECK instead of operator[] in
   DestroyConfigurationInternal.
4. Erase any previous service_path and unregister the observer when
   updating a configuration's service_path in RegisterConfiguration.

      --gtest_filter="VpnProviderApiTest.*"

tree
59088eb51116d4c6adf8e2ff67d20c06383b71b8
parent
55a38d8bb3d644b6e9e6a4cad9c94e8c022861d2 [ diff ] 
Ash: Migrate ArcNetHostImplTest off RunUntilIdle

This changelist is part of an ongoing series to modernize Ash tests by
migrating them away from RunUntilIdle() and toward targeted
synchronization with APIs such as RunUntil(), TestFuture, RunLoop
callbacks, observer waiters, or exact mock-time advancement.

ArcNetHostImplTest now waits for the logged-in user's Shill profile and
completion of the network policy applications before creating networks.
Policy application starts only after the profile's asynchronous load,
so readiness requires both the cached profile and completed policy
application. Fake Shill replies and policy tasks wake the predicate on
the test sequence.

parent
6f403a7947ac5437d8b6399507ecb9377622c3a6 [ diff ] 
[Public Transit] Wait for hit test data when entering page Stations

Page Stations waited for PageInteractableOrHiddenCondition, but not
PageHitTestDataCondition, so they waited for less than
ChromeTabUtils#waitForInteractable(). Add it to BasePageStation so both
wait for the same state, and deprecate waitForInteractable() in favor of
page Stations.

PageHitTestDataCondition is fulfilled right away for hidden tabs, native
pages, about:blank (since crrev.com/c/8705639) and uncommitted initial
navigations, so only visible web pages wait for hit test data. It uses
the Trip timeout instead of the 10s waitForInteractable() used.

b60b272510c2b56a69f304042f5471b7a706bf52 [ diff ] 
Add TestMemoryConsumerRegistry::NotifyUpdateMemoryLimitAndRelease()

Many tests simulate memory pressure by updating the memory limit and
then requesting a memory release, which mirrors what the memory
coordinator does in production. For async consumers, this required
passing base::DoNothing() to NotifyUpdateMemoryLimitAsync() and relying
on main thread task ordering for the release to be observed after the
limit update.

This CL adds NotifyUpdateMemoryLimitAndRelease() and its async variant
to TestMemoryConsumerRegistry, and migrates all existing callers that
issue both notifications back-to-back. Tests that check state between
the two notifications are left unchanged.

This is a mechanical, test-only change.

tree
723b0ea925690aa0374d5f5585441b358b85bed1
parent
934da7ece55f3e1be03c2a37ec569ecf17ada34e [ diff ] 
[Linux] Only use the OpenURI portal in OpenExternal() when sandboxed

https://crrev.com/c/7694233 switched `platform_util::OpenExternal()` on
Linux to prefer `org.freedesktop.portal.OpenURI` whenever
`org.freedesktop.portal.Desktop` is available on D-Bus.

`xdg-desktop-portal` resolves URI schemes exclusively through `.desktop`
MIME associations (`x-scheme-handler/<scheme>`). On unsandboxed host
installs where a scheme is handled only by a custom `xdg-open` script,
`$BROWSER` or mailcap, the portal shows an empty "No Apps available"
chooser instead. Dismissing it emits `Response(1)`
(`kRequestCancelledByUser`), so there is no fallback to `xdg-open` and
the link silently does nothing.

The portal is needed only inside Flatpak and Snap sandboxes, where
`xdg-open` and `xdg-email` may be unavailable or can't reach the host's
handlers. Restore the pre-M148 behavior on host installs: when
`base::nix::GetAppSandbox()` returns `kNone`, `OpenExternal()` uses
`xdg-open` / `xdg-email` directly without touching D-Bus. Sandboxed
installs keep using `OpenURI` / `OpenFile`.

8101abcc2811919ea617952e4e88530f6e9d7110 [ diff ] 
Add screen reader announcement when reordering shortcuts

This change introduces an aria-live region to announce position changes
when a user moves a shortcut tile using keyboard navigation.

- Hides the drag icon from the a11y tree.
- Replaces map() with repeat() so when moving a tile it keeps the same
  DOM node for screen readers, and awaits for updateComplete before
  applying force-hover on drop so the hover state lands on the moved
  tile.
- Returns early when attempting to move a single tile so that a no-op
  move is not announced.

screencast/cast/NTM2NjcwNTY2NzcwMjc4NHw5NGNkOTE1NC1jZQ#t=15

5c6e99ba4e9d9fecdf53059d79e22825d803e23b [ diff ] 
Archive devtools-frontend.zip produced by devtools_frontend_zip

DevTools now builds devtools-frontend.zip itself via the
devtools_frontend_zip GN target, with a layout that is independent of
where DevTools lives in the source tree (all files under
devtools-frontend/front_end/).

  * Upload the prebuilt $root_out_dir/devtools-frontend.zip from the
    *-archive-rel builders instead of zipping the raw
    gen/third_party/devtools-frontend/src/front_end directory. These
    builders compile "all", so the zip is built without further changes.
  * Add //third_party/blink/public:blink_devtools_frontend_zip, which
    forwards to the zip target via devtools_root_location. The official
    archive configs use it as SBOM GN target, so the label stays stable
    when DevTools moves to third_party/devtools.

With this, nothing needs to change here when DevTools moves.

Note: this changes the layout of the devtools-frontend.zip snapshots in
chromium-browser-snapshots from devtools-frontend/gen/third_party/
devtools-frontend/src/front_end/ to devtools-frontend/front_end/, and
the zip only contains the files the remote DevTools server serves.

[MemoryCoordinator] Remove the SuppressMemoryListeners experiment

The suppression masks of kSuppressMemoryListeners no longer match any
registered MemoryPressureListener: every suppressed tag is either
deprecated or no longer used by any MemoryPressureListenerRegistration.

Remove the feature, its mask parameter and the associated tests. This
is a no-op behavior-wise.

[webium] Prerender the toolbar for a profile's next window

Design: https://docs.google.com/document/d/1lzWRQwj2u4QItO-Wd3BbhoJbvc7dIxLFBpZeGumxEmw

This is Phase 1 of prerendering for TopChrome WebUI, gated by the new
`TopChromeWebUIPrerendering` feature.

Prerendering builds on profile prewarm. Today there are 2 types of
different prewarm existing for webui toolbar:

1. Profile prewarm (`WebUIReloadButtonPrewarmWebUI` +
   `WebUIReloadButtonProfilePrewarming`): InitialWebUIProfileService
   builds one toolbar when the profile loads, and only the profile's
   first window adopts it. This is the one this CL depends on.
2. Per-window prewarm (`WebUIReloadButtonPrewarmWebUI`): Each
   window's InitialWebUIManager builds its toolbar when the browser
   is constructed. Replaced by this CL.

With this CL, a profile keeps one prerendered WebUI toolbar, i.e. a
spare, ready for its next window, and builds a new spare shortly
after each window takes one. Every new window of the profile, not only
the first, then paints its toolbar sooner.

The prerendering logic lives in a reusable WebUIContentsPrerenderer,
so later top-chrome WebUIs can prerender the same way. A spare loads
its page ahead of time only with
`WebUIReloadButtonPrewarmWebUIPreNavigate` on; without it, a spare
only starts its renderer.

Background

Today, every window after a profile's first, with either prewarm mode or
neither, creates, navigates, and runs its toolbar while the window is
being built. That delays the window's first paint, most on low-core
machines and low-end devices.

Structure

The prerendering mechanics are generic, and the profile binding stays in
the per-profile service, so later top-chrome components and the Phase 2
spare with no user profile can reuse the mechanics.

- WebUIContentsPrerenderer, in chrome/browser/ui/webui/top_chrome/,
  keeps one spare for one WebUI. It drops a spare whose renderer is
  gone or that already painted, and schedules the next spare after each
  take. It has no toolbar or profile types: a delegate builds each spare
  and decides whether what the spare read is still current.
- InitialWebUIProfileService is the toolbar's delegate and owns the
  prerenderer for its profile. It builds the spare, records what the
  toolbar read when it navigated, maps the feature's params, records
  the take histogram, and drops the spare with the profile's last
  normal window.

Scheduling

With TopChromeWebUIPrerendering on, InitialWebUIProfileService builds no
toolbar at profile creation, so startup and session-restore windows
build their own toolbars, as with prewarm off. Each window's take
schedules the next spare instead. The prerenderer waits until the
adopted spare paints, or for TopChromeWebUIPrerenderingPaintDeadline
(default 3 s) when the window built its own toolbar. It then waits for
TopChromeWebUIPrerenderingDelay (default 2 s), which keeps the build
away from the new window's first frames and the user's first input, and
builds the spare. A later take restarts the wait. Both values were
picked rather than derived, so they are params. The prerenderer skips
the build under the memory pressure level that
TopChromeWebUIPrerenderingMemoryPressure names: critical by default, or
moderate.

The wait follows WebUIContentsPreloadManager::PendingPreload. The
toolbar does not reuse the preload manager itself, because that manager
keeps one WebUI for all profiles, while each profile needs its own
toolbar, dropped with its last window and checked when a window adopts
it.

Per-window prewarm stays off with prerendering on. It starts the build
only slightly earlier, showed no gain for new windows, and would put
startup windows on the adoption path that prerendering uses only for
spares.

Adoption checks

A spare can wait a long time before a window takes it, so the take
checks that it still fits. The window drops the spare and builds its
toolbar inline, as with prewarm off, when the spare's renderer is gone,
or when its colors, Glic or avatar button eligibility, home button pref,
or touch mode changed since it was built. It also drops a spare that
already painted, because the window would then miss
DidFirstVisuallyNonEmptyPaint and its surface sync deadline would not
clear. The prerenderer makes the renderer and paint checks, and the
service makes the other checks, since they depend on what the toolbar
reads.

Lifetime

The service resets the prerenderer when the profile's last normal window
closes or the service shuts down. A dropped spare is destroyed right
away. The new histogram InitialWebUI.Toolbar.ProfilePrewarmTakeResult
records the outcome of each take.

Cost

Each profile with an open window holds one extra toolbar, and each new
window triggers one background toolbar build.

Revert "[devtools] Add GetBrowserWindowCaptureDevice() for Chrome"

This reverts commit 719fb77cad92f8e7f3ac897fb3787734f3c081ef.

Reason for revert: Break linux-wayland CI

Failure Link: https://ci.chromium.org/ui/p/chromium/builders/ci/linux-wayland-mutter-rel-tests/30144/overview

Original change's description:
> [devtools] Add GetBrowserWindowCaptureDevice() for Chrome
>
> Page.startScreenRecording with captureArea: browserWindow must record
> the browser window that shows the target. Only the embedder knows which
> window that is, so content asks it.
>
> Add DevToolsManagerDelegate::GetBrowserWindowCaptureDevice(). The
> embedder returns a BrowserWindowCaptureDevice: the DesktopMediaID of the
> window, as in a getDisplayMedia() response, and an optional
> MediaStreamUI that shows that the window is captured. The default
> implementation fails, so headless and content_shell return an error, as
> before.
>
> PageHandler asks the delegate for the device, because that decides
> which window the client may record, and starts the recording with
> MediaRecorder::StartDisplayCapture(). Audio comes from the target, as
> when only the target is recorded. If Page.stopScreenRecording runs
> before the embedder answers, it fails, and the start command fails too.
>
> Implement the delegate in Chrome with the parts that getDisplayMedia()
> uses for window capture:
>
> - For a docked DevTools window, use the browser window of the inspected
>   tab, which shows DevTools and the page. An undocked DevTools window is
>   not supported, because the page is in another window. For a tab
>   target, use the browser window of the tab.
> - Respect the ScreenCaptureAllowed policy like getDisplayMedia() window
>   capture. The check uses an empty origin, because a CDP client and not
>   the inspected page asks for the recording. Thus a specific origin in
>   the per-origin lists does not apply, but the "*" pattern does.
> - Create the DesktopMediaID of the browser window like the picker does
>   for Chrome's own windows, so that the launcher uses the viz-based
>   window capture device.
> - Call GetDevicesForDesktopCapture(), which returns the device and a
>   MediaStreamUI that registers with MediaStreamCaptureIndicator and
>   shows the screen sharing notification with a Stop button. The
>   notification names the product, for example "Google Chrome is sharing
>   a window".
>
> The recording shows what the window shows. If the target is in a
> background tab, or if the user selects another tab, the recording shows
> the active tab.
>
> There is no picker and no user activation, because the use case is
> automation over a trusted CDP connection.
>
> The browser tests run on Aura (including ChromeOS) and Mac. They decode
> the recording in a <video> element and check that the video shows the
> whole window with the page for a DevTools target and a tab target (also
> with docked DevTools, which is how a client records the page's audio),
> that the video follows the window size after a resize, that the
> indicator follows the recording, that Stop in the UI (also a click on
> the real Stop button of the notification) stops the capture but keeps
> the stream, the error cases (undocked DevTools, untrusted client,
> policy), and how the per-origin policy lists apply.
> Page.startScreenRecording answers after the first frame, so the tests
> have no fixed waits.
>
> TAG=agy
> CONV=b053a892-aea6-49aa-9d08-a76b1505a839
>

Require and expose embeddingSpace in SemanticEmbedder

Embeddings are only comparable within one embedding space, so pages
need to name the space they want and each result needs to say which
space it is in.

This CL makes four changes:

1. Require options with an embeddingSpace in availability() and
   create(). An unsupported space is "unavailable", and create()
   rejects with NotSupportedError.
2. Expose SemanticEmbedder.embeddingSpace and a required embeddingSpace
   on each ContentEmbedding, and mark ContentEmbedding.values and
   SemanticEmbedderResult.embeddings required.
3. Pass the space in a new AISemanticEmbedderCreateOptions struct to
   CanCreateSemanticEmbedder() and CreateSemanticEmbedder(), and check
   it against a new table of supported spaces ("embeddinggemma-300m").
   For an unknown space, CanCreateSemanticEmbedder() returns the new
   kUnavailableUnsupportedEmbeddingSpace and CreateSemanticEmbedder()
   reports a bad message.
4. Add the SemanticEmbedder_EmbeddingSpace UseCounter, and update the
   content_shell echo, the web tests, AISemanticEmbedderBrowserTest and
   a comment in ai_on_device_interactive_uitest.cc.

Fail jobs whose embeddings can't be normalized instead of crashing
📋 Metadata

32d10af7547431e46e5066928e006088ed4203dd

tree
ba059c0f77671e8952ad4796952b93f97dcc8a18
parent
a59377a31730703ed0103ae8b0752201710b39d6 [ diff ] 
Fail jobs whose embeddings can't be normalized instead of crashing

The embeddings process is less trusted than the browser, but
SchedulingEmbedder assumes every vector it returns can be scaled to
unit length. An empty or all-zero vector reaches value() on an empty
optional and crashes the browser, and a vector with NaN or infinite
values, or one whose sum of squares overflows, is passed on as garbage
in builds without DCHECKs.

This CL makes three changes:

1. Make Embedding::Normalize() also return nullopt when the vector's
   magnitude isn't finite.
2. Stop reading a batch's results at the first embedding that can't be
   normalized. Jobs before it keep their results, the job that owns it
   fails, and jobs after it go back to the queue.
3. Add tests for empty, zero, non-finite and overflowing vectors, for a
   bad vector in the middle of a batch, and for a bad extra vector after
   the last passage.

tree
68ce6b9d0024cc3274231535474e56f19c15248e
parent
d6cd92d02bb44c93aa5b9a466fea829f2b10ed9b [ diff ] 
[DCSI] Fold SharedMemoryCopyStrategy into CPU copy strategy

SharedMemoryCopyStrategy only handled 1-hop CPU upload/readback between
a SharedMemoryImageBacking and a non-shared-memory backing using
SharedMemoryImageBacking::pixmaps(). Fold that fast path directly into
CPUReadbackUploadCopyStrategy and remove SharedMemoryCopyStrategy:

- When either src_backing or dst_backing is kSharedMemory,
  CPUReadbackUploadCopyStrategy::Copy() uploads from or reads back into
  SharedMemoryImageBacking::pixmaps() directly in 1 hop without
  allocating intermediate SkBitmap staging buffers.
- Reject kSharedMemory-to-kSharedMemory in CanCopy() and Copy() since
  CompoundImageBacking holds at most one shared memory backing and
  SharedMemoryImageBacking does not implement ReadbackToMemory() or
  UploadFromMemory().
- Retain the 2-hop temporary SkBitmap readback + upload fallback when
  neither backing is kSharedMemory.

parent
d1dd0d00b7c40cb6f028108b394096a22eb2fa13 [ diff ] 
CrOS Settings: Deflake per-device keyboard subsection test

The "remap keys sub-label" test reads a label that PluralStringProxy
fills in by asking the browser process. The test only waits for one
flushTasks(), so sometimes the reply hasn't arrived yet and the label
is still empty.

Use a fake proxy that answers right away. PluralStringProxyImpl is now
exported from os_settings.js so the test can swap it in. Importing it
directly wouldn't work in optimized builds, where the module is bundled.

Bug: b:337910908
Test: browser_tests --gtest_filter=*DevicePagePerDeviceKeyboardSubsection* --gtest_repeat=50 --test-launcher-retry-limit=0

Ash: Migrate ProximityAuthSystemTest off RunUntilIdle

This changelist is part of an ongoing series to modernize Ash tests by
migrating them away from RunUntilIdle() and toward targeted
synchronization with APIs such as RunUntil(), TestFuture, RunLoop
callbacks, observer waiters, or exact mock-time advancement.

ProximityAuthSystemTest simulates suspend and resume with direct calls
to ProximityAuthSystem. These calls update the fake remote-device life
cycle and notify the mock unlock manager before returning, so the
existing assertions need no idle drain. Remove the now-unused task
runner and its current-default handle as well.

Add a 3pp package for the snap runtime libraries

The snap package bundles a few Ubuntu 26.04 libraries that the core26
base does not provide (NSS, libsecret, opensc-pkcs11, Qt 6). Today
snapcraft fetches them as stage-packages, which needs snapcraft and
snapd on the builders and is not pinned.

Add //chrome/installer/linux/snap/runtime/3pp, which the
3pp-linux-amd64-packager builder uploads to CIPD as
chromium/chrome/installer/linux/snap/runtime/linux-amd64:
- update.py (developer tool) resolves the packages in config.json with
  apt-get at a snapshot.ubuntu.com timestamp. apt runs in a private
  root with the core26 base packages marked as installed, checks the
  archive signature against the Ubuntu archive signing key (2018) and
  downloads the .debs. update.py records their URLs, sizes and SHA256s
  in runtime.lock.json, along with the files the gnome extension's
  cleanup parts would remove (libraries provided by the content snaps).
- build.py only reads runtime.lock.json: it downloads the pinned .debs,
  checks their SHA256s, extracts them and applies the prime filters of
  the snapcraft parts and the cleanup list.

The man page part (man-db, groff) is not included. The DEPS entry
(gated on a new checkout_snap_runtime gclient var, checked out to
runtime/linux-amd64) and the build.py changes follow once the builder
has uploaded a version.

Verified locally: at snapshot 20261103T000000Z, update.py resolves the
same packages as snapcraft's stage-packages in an Ubuntu 26.04 VM, except for the empty transitional package lsb-base (provided
by sysvinit-utils in core26). The installed files match snapcraft's
prime directory except for the man page part and one pkg-config file
that snapcraft rewrites.

parent
e7c18824fca91760d2a3a72cd5e2604c86d8390c [ diff ] 
[BlockModel] Guard block model for encrypted playback separately

In the MediaCodecLinearBlock study, enabling block model makes
Widevine H.264 playback fail with PIPELINE_ERROR_DECODE for 79% of L3
clients (0.5% in Control) and 19% of L1 clients (0% in Control),
while clear H.264 is flat.

Add kMediaCodecBlockModelEncrypted, disabled by default, and have
MediaCodecVideoDecoder turn block model off once a MediaCrypto
arrives unless that feature is enabled. The check keys on MediaCrypto
rather than the decoder config because the codec gets the MediaCrypto
even for clear configs and is reused if the stream turns encrypted.

This lets the clear-content study resume while the encrypted failures
are root-caused, and lets the encrypted fix run as its own experiment.

  played Widevine L3 H.264 (cenc and cbcs) with and without
  MediaCodecBlockModelEncrypted. Logcat showed block_model=false
  without the feature and block_model=true with it, and every run
  played without errors.

Bug: 582399297, 327625558
Test: On an Android 17 QPR2 device with MediaCodecBlockModel enabled,

Ash: Migrate ArcUtilTest off RunUntilIdle

This changelist is part of an ongoing series to modernize Ash tests by
migrating them away from RunUntilIdle() and toward targeted
synchronization with APIs such as RunUntil(), TestFuture, RunLoop
callbacks, observer waiters, or exact mock-time advancement.

ArcUtilTest already waits for the stale ARCVM shutdown callback.
The helper processes every Upstart job before requesting the VM stop
from Concierge, then completes from its reply. Remove the redundant idle
drain before checking the recorded jobs and VM-stop count.

🌺 Revert "[TabStateStore] Remove WebContentsState#destroy via GC direct buffers"

This reverts commit 3adaab964b44c2826522f8415c24c7629eee56d1.
Culprit: 3adaab964b44 - "[TabStateStore] Remove WebContentsState#destroy via GC direct buffers" (refs/heads/main@{#1736962}, https://crrev.com/c/8730243)

Reason for revert: consistently fails android-arm-tests.

Mechanism: Commit 3adaab964b44c2826522f8415c24c7629eee56d1 (r1736962)
removed WebContentsState's copy constructor and changed
TabStateExtractor#getWebContentsState to directly return existingState
(tab.getWebContentsState()) without updating the
assertNotSame(oldContentsState, restoredTab.getWebContentsState())
assertion in TabArchiverTest.java:1352, causing
restoredTab.getWebContentsState() to be the exact same object reference
as oldContentsState and failing assertNotSame.

org.chromium.chrome.browser.tab.TabArchiverTest#testUnarchiveAndRestoreTabs_RestoreFrozenTabWithWebContentsState
has failed in 3/3 recent builds of android-arm-tests (#40947, #40948,
#40949), every attempt in every one of them. This is not flake: it
reproduces on every run, so it blocks the CQ for every unrelated CL that
touches this suite. The regression appears at refs/heads/main@{#1736962}.

Example failing run:
https://ci.chromium.org/ui/p/chrome/builders/ci/android-arm-tests/40949

To run/deflake this test on CQ before relanding, add the following
footer to your CL description:
  Include-Ci-Only-Tests: official.chrome.continuous:android-arm-tests|chrome_public_test_apk

Reverting to green the tree first. Please re-land once the failure is
understood and covered; this revert is about restoring CQ throughput,
not about the merit of the change.

Original change's description:
> [TabStateStore] Remove WebContentsState#destroy via GC direct buffers
>
> Previously, loaded tabs kept serialized WebContentsState bytes in C++
> std::strings wrapped by JNI NewDirectByteBuffer. Because the Java GC did
> not own those C++ strings, callers had to manually track and free them
> via LoadedTabState#claim(), LoadedTabState#destroy(), and
> WebContentsState#destroy().
>
> Instead, copy each tab's state bytes on the background DB sequence into
> a Java-owned direct ByteBuffer (ByteBuffer.allocateDirect, matching all
> other WebContentsState factories) and immediately free the C++
> std::string. Once in a DirectByteBuffer, the Android GC frees each tab's
> buffer automatically when its WebContentsState becomes unreachable,
> allowing WebContentsState#destroy() and LoadedTabState#claim()/destroy()
> to be removed while keeping WebContentsState immutable wrt its buffer
> (with appendPendingNavigation and deleteNavigationEntries returning new
> WebContentsState instances).
>
> StorageLoadedData itself remains Destroyable because Java's mNativePtr
> (JniUniquePtr) is the single owner of C++ StorageLoadedDataAndroid; if
> an in-flight background load is cancelled before reaching the UI thread,
> a ScopedClosureRunner destroys the Java wrapper to free its owned C++
> peer. Marshalling StorageLoadedData on the DB sequence also cuts 100-tab
> UI-thread loadAllData work on Android x64 to 0.14-0.15 ms (down 90-92%
> vs. the UI-thread baseline).
>
> Bug: 581427066
> Change-Id: I8164258b31bcb5140687b2de48cf09086a6a6964
> Reviewed-on: https://chromium-review.googlesource.com/c/chromium/src/+/8730243
> Commit-Queue: Calder Kitagawa <ckitagawa@chromium.org>
> Reviewed-by: Fiaz Muhammad <mfiaz@google.com>
> Cr-Commit-Position: refs/heads/main@{#1736962}

Bug: 581427066

🌺 Revert "Enable FractionalScrollOffsets by default"

This reverts commit 569472b687410bc81d127529e31fe7d213b038ef.
Culprit: 569472b68741 - "Enable FractionalScrollOffsets by default" (refs/heads/main@{#1737074}, https://crrev.com/c/8485405)

Reason for revert: consistently fails android-16-x64-dbg-tests and
android-16-x64-rel.

Mechanism: Commit 569472b687410bc81d127529e31fe7d213b038ef (r1737074)
promoted the Blink FractionalScrollOffsets runtime feature from
"experimental" to "stable" in
third_party/blink/renderer/platform/runtime_enabled_features.json5. In
ScrollCaptureCallbackRenderTest#testCaptureBottom, the test scrolls
/chrome/test/data/android/share/checkerboard.html to the bottom via
scrollBy(0, offset), and ScrollCaptureCallbackDelegate#onCompositorReady
converts scrollOffset.y into physical pixel coordinates via
mInitialYOffset = (int) Math.floor(scrollOffset.y *
mMinPageScaleFactor). With FractionalScrollOffsets enabled by default,
Blink preserves the fractional scroll offset rather than truncating to
whole pixels, shifting the computed mInitialYOffset and all 7 captured
bottom viewport slices (scroll_capture_bottom_viewport_0 through
scroll_capture_bottom_viewport_-6) and causing deterministic Skia Gold
comparison failures (rev_2). Please either bump/triage the
ScrollCaptureCallbackRenderTest Skia Gold revision or update the scroll
offset rounding in ScrollCaptureCallbackDelegate /
ScrollCaptureCallbackRenderTest before relanding.

org.chromium.chrome.browser.share.scroll_capture.ScrollCaptureCallbackRenderTest#testCaptureBottom
has failed in 3/3 recent builds across android-16-x64-dbg-tests and
android-16-x64-rel, every attempt in every one of them. This is not
flake: it reproduces on every run, so it blocks the CQ for every
unrelated CL that touches this suite. The regression appears at
refs/heads/main@{#1737074}.

Example failing runs:
https://ci.chromium.org/ui/p/chromium/builders/ci/android-16-x64-dbg-tests/9938
https://ci.chromium.org/ui/p/chromium/builders/ci/android-16-x64-rel/7953

To run/deflake this test on CQ before relanding, add the following
footer to your CL description:
  Include-Ci-Only-Tests: chromium.android:android-16-x64-dbg-tests,chromium.android:android-16-x64-rel|chrome_public_test_apk

Reverting to green the tree first. Please re-land once the failure is
understood and covered; this revert is about restoring CQ throughput,
not about the merit of the change.

Original change's description:
> Enable FractionalScrollOffsets by default
>
> Promote the runtime feature from experimental to stable.
>
> Base web tests already run with the feature enabled, so the
> fractional-scroll-offsets virtual suite is inverted rather than removed:
> it becomes fractional-scroll-offsets-disabled, running the same tests
> with --disable-blink-features=FractionalScrollOffsets so the disabled
> path keeps coverage while the flag is still around. Baselines for the
> disabled run of repaint-during-scroll-with-zoom.html are added, since
> that path rounds the scroll offset to whole physical pixels.
>
> NavigationControllerBrowserTest's scroll expectations move into a
> VerifyScrollOffset helper: with the feature disabled the offset is
> truncated to a physical pixel, while with it enabled the page scale
> factor can still quantize the offset, so an exact CSS pixel expectation
> does not hold on Android.
>
> Bug: 40732416
> Change-Id: I0916bfe09980b2d222be2ba329778f8d67e28db7
> Reviewed-on: https://chromium-review.googlesource.com/c/chromium/src/+/8485405
> Commit-Queue: Helmut Januschka <helmut@januschka.com>
> Auto-Submit: Helmut Januschka <helmut@januschka.com>
> Reviewed-by: Jonathan Ross <jonross@chromium.org>
> Reviewed-by: David Bokan <bokan@chromium.org>

2f3e6c7d717743d03485fc40b01ab17d095de5af

committer

Fri Nov 06 05:13:25 2026
tree
65fca542b4922d5db52aa53873d32bd5438177f1
parent
d8fc2a1aef8499476b5b37d135a055a7e7d4a0ed [ diff ] 
ash: Move ChromeOS log file utilities to //chromeos/ash/components/logging

chrome/common/logging_chrome.{h,cc} contains several ChromeOS-only
utilities to manage log files (rotation, symlinks, timestamped names,
session log directory). They are used from //chrome/browser/ash, which
makes //chrome/browser/ash depend on //chrome/common.

This CL moves them to a new component //chromeos/ash/components/logging,
which depends only on //base, so that both chrome/common (for
InitChromeLogging()) and ash code can use them.

- GenerateTimestampedName, RotateLogFile, SetUpSymlinkIfNeeded,
  SetUpLogFile and RemoveSymlinkAndLog are moved to log_file_util.h.
- GetSessionLogDir and the CHROMEOS_SESSION_LOG_DIR env var name
  (env_vars::kSessionLogDir) are moved to session_log.h.
  GetSessionLogDir no longer takes an unused CommandLine argument.
- The corresponding unit tests are moved from
  chrome/browser/logging_chrome_unittest.cc.

The callers in //chrome/browser/ash and //chrome/browser/ui/webui/ash
are updated to use the new component.

No behavior change is intended.

tree
6e724cc7e1f225647aed063e21e13f88d7222649
parent
246a3dd98708a33199784720a97066eb736bd2a3 [ diff ] 
blink: Skip UpdateMixins() when CSSMixins is disabled

@mixin rules are only parsed when CSSMixins is enabled, but
UpdateMixins() still walks every stylesheet (including @media and
@supports subtrees) on every active stylesheet update.

On a Celeron N4000 Chromebook (ui.PageLoadPerf), skipping this saves
16.5 ms on the renderer main thread and reduces LCP by ~5% (median
~9.5%).

tree
4d244688060d798e15e4273df9aea01119f77cd9
parent
3df5bf04d7721d882709b869f1871143da5d1034 [ diff ] 
WebNN: Copy compiled CoreML model directly without base::Move

Have the compiler process move CoreML's compiled output directly to
`$TMPDIR/webnn_compiler_protected/<scoped_dir_A>` without an extra
`model.mlmodelc` subdirectory. The browser process can then copy
`<scoped_dir_A>` directly to
`$TMPDIR/webnn_gpu_protected/<scoped_dir_B>/model.mlmodelc` using
`base::CopyDirectoryNoFollow`, avoiding `base::Move` (which falls back
to symlink-following `base::CopyDirectory` on failure) and
`base::DeletePathRecursively`.

[CodeHealth] Remove remaining Pepper comments and docs in media/

After PPAPI was removed in M139, `docs/media/audio_focus.md` still
included an obsolete "Handling Pepper" section, and class comments
for `BitstreamBuffer`, `Picture`, and `VideoDecodeAccelerator` still
described them as equivalents or backends for removed Pepper types
(`PP_VideoBitstreamBuffer_Dev`, `PP_Picture_Dev`, and
`PPB_VideoDecoder_Dev`).

This CL removes the Pepper section from the audio focus documentation
and strips the obsolete Pepper type references from the class comments
in `media/`.

f2f89f7ad881e27cc4f29dbb55a2563367bf012d
parent
aa9c62bf3f1779e25d26b97b6835bf5068827c55 [ diff ] 
[ChroMenu] Show live zoom level on zoom in/out search results

Zoom in/out search results keep the menu open when activated and the
zoom percentage is no longer a result of its own, so users had no
indication of the current level. Show it on the rows' secondary line
("Zoom - 110%") and keep it current.

BrowserCommandController now sets a new chrome::kActionSecondaryTextKey
on the two zoom in/out actions with the formatted secondary text. The
property change notifies the rows through their existing
ActionViewController binding, and AppMenuItemActionViewInterface renders
the property when present or falls back to the row's default breadcrumb.
Each row stays bound to a single action.

tree
8977a2b713a13bf4992ab07370df13524421dcd2
parent
55c9185ea108569a48f5f67e3f59e57576bfd001 [ diff ] 
Migrate device chooser dialogs to JniRawPtr

Passing native device chooser dialog pointers across JNI as raw
longs bypasses type safety and leaves borrowed reference lifetimes
implicit until closeDialog() is invoked.

Migrate UsbChooserDialog, SerialChooserDialog, and HidChooserDialog
to hold borrowed JniRawPtr handles released in closeDialog() and on
early returns, and pass JniPtr to native methods.

e3c85ba6f9cfdc1670caaace119224e2d03f5f7d
parent
3aba84f2dff1b96c5520efa84a76cfe2a5290a09 [ diff ] 
Ash: Add a media capture dispatcher interface in components/webrtc

This CL is part of an ongoing series to remove Ash code's dependency
on MediaCaptureDevicesDispatcher, a //chrome singleton, by moving Ash
callers to an interface in components/webrtc that the dispatcher
implements.

When Ash web content, such as the Camera app, the login screen or an
embedded web view, asks to use the camera or microphone, the dispatcher
decides whether to grant it and tells observers about the request.
This CL adds the interface for that role. Ash callers move to it in
follow-up CLs.

tree
8141446108c7a05014ea43eb7a81d969ab3b7f33
parent
8f1acbfae8c25f37c83d7de494a73b4c6cb0bea4 [ diff ] 
[ttc] Show pipeline stage health in TtcDebugSession

Rates each stage of the TTC audio pipeline (mic, encode, send, server,
receive, speaker) as idle, ok, warning or error from how recently its
signals were seen: no mic audio or digital silence, chunks not sent or
dropped, transport loss or server errors, and reply audio that arrives
but isn't played (unless an interruption dropped it). The result is
part of every snapshot and export, so the debug page can show at a
glance where the pipeline stalls.

Part 6 of the chrome://ttc-internals series. Design: go/ttc-internals

3e5cc433780c95cdbc1506a1d76038e5e66dc1aa
parent
90ec08de19a5782e6c9032029a31d7518de93c54 [ diff ] 
Track latency gen204 IDs per LatencyType in TestLensOverlayQueryController

When OpenLensOverlay runs on a webpage, both the full-image request and
the page-content upload request execute concurrently on background
thread-pool task runners. When PageContentResponseHandler completes on
the UI thread, MaybeSendPageContentUploadLatencyGen204 calls
SendLatencyGen204IfEnabled with LatencyType::kPageContentUploadLatency
and a request ID whose media_type is MEDIA_TYPE_WEBPAGE_AND_IMAGE.
Because TestLensOverlayQueryController stored only a single scalar
last_latency_gen204_request_id_ and last_latency_gen204_analytics_id_,
kPageContentUploadLatency completing after
kFullPageObjectsRequestFetchLatency or kInteractionRequestFetchLatency
overwrote the request/analytics ID checked by
CorrectAnalyticsAndRequestIdSentWithGen204s.

Also store latency gen204 request and analytics IDs per LatencyType and
check the specific LatencyType in
CorrectAnalyticsAndRequestIdSentWithGen204s.

Test history:
https://ci.chromium.org/ui/test/chromium/%3A%2F%2Fchrome%2Ftest%5C%3Abrowser_tests%21gtest%3A%3ALensOverlayControllerBrowserTest%23CorrectAnalyticsAndRequestIdSentWithGen204s

ash: Move policy-controlled CrOS prefs to ash/constants/ash_pref_names.h

The following ChromeOS-only prefs are registered by ash::Preferences
and are controlled by enterprise policy, but their names were defined
in chrome/common/pref_names.h:
- kUnifiedDesktopEnabledByDefault
- kAllowExcludeDisplayInMirrorMode
- kCastReceiverEnabled
- kStartupBrowserWindowLaunchSuppressed

Move them to ash/constants/ash_pref_names.h (ash::prefs) and update
the users, including the policy handler list, the command line pref
store, settings_private's prefs_util and StartupBrowserCreator.
Pref name string values are unchanged, so no migration is needed.

02023f18db9d4f41f42d66e1cadc48532e22a607 [ diff ] 
[devtools] Add GetBrowserWindowCaptureDevice() for Chrome

Page.startScreenRecording with captureArea: browserWindow must record
the browser window that shows the target. Only the embedder knows which
window that is, so content asks it.

Add DevToolsManagerDelegate::GetBrowserWindowCaptureDevice(). The
embedder returns a BrowserWindowCaptureDevice: the DesktopMediaID of the
window, as in a getDisplayMedia() response, and an optional
MediaStreamUI that shows that the window is captured. The default
implementation fails, so headless and content_shell return an error, as
before.

PageHandler asks the delegate for the device, because that decides
which window the client may record, and starts the recording with
MediaRecorder::StartDisplayCapture(). Audio comes from the target, as
when only the target is recorded. If Page.stopScreenRecording runs
before the embedder answers, it fails, and the start command fails too.

Implement the delegate in Chrome with the parts that getDisplayMedia()
uses for window capture:

- For a docked DevTools window, use the browser window of the inspected
  tab, which shows DevTools and the page. An undocked DevTools window is
  not supported, because the page is in another window. For a tab
  target, use the browser window of the tab.
- Respect the ScreenCaptureAllowed policy like getDisplayMedia() window
  capture. The check uses an empty origin, because a CDP client and not
  the inspected page asks for the recording. Thus a specific origin in
  the per-origin lists does not apply, but the "*" pattern does.
- Create the DesktopMediaID of the browser window like the picker does
  for Chrome's own windows, so that the launcher uses the viz-based
  window capture device.
- Call GetDevicesForDesktopCapture(), which returns the device and a
  MediaStreamUI that registers with MediaStreamCaptureIndicator and
  shows the screen sharing notification with a Stop button. The
  notification names the product, for example "Google Chrome is sharing
  a window".

The recording shows what the window shows. If the target is in a
background tab, or if the user selects another tab, the recording shows
the active tab.

There is no picker and no user activation, because the use case is
automation over a trusted CDP connection.

The browser tests run on Aura (including ChromeOS) and Mac. They decode
the recording in a <video> element and check that the video shows the
whole window with the page for a DevTools target and a tab target (also
with docked DevTools, which is how a client records the page's audio),
that the video follows the window size after a resize, that the
indicator follows the recording, that Stop in the UI (also a click on
the real Stop button of the notification) stops the capture but keeps
the stream, the error cases (undocked DevTools, untrusted client,
policy), and how the per-origin policy lists apply.
Page.startScreenRecording answers after the first frame, so the tests
have no fixed waits.

TAG=agy
CONV=b053a892-aea6-49aa-9d08-a76b1505a839

b73a63029656f2514aad3c1e16e0134f92ecfcde [ diff ] 
Decouple Quick Pair repository from ash/shell.h via local_state DI

Under Siso's fail-on-bad-deps check, obj/ash/quick_pair/repository/
repository/device_address_map.o and device_image_store.o failed due to
missing dependencies on generated mojom headers
(ui/accessibility:ax_enums_mojo and ui/base/cursor/mojom:cursor_type).

This occurred because DeviceAddressMap and DeviceImageStore called
Shell::Get()->local_state(), causing //ash/quick_pair/repository to
include ash/shell.h and transitively pull in UI headers (ui/views,
ui/wm) without declaring direct GN dependencies.

This CL fixes the bad-deps violations at the root cause by:
- Injecting PrefService& local_state into DeviceAddressMap,
  DeviceImageStore, and FastPairRepositoryImpl via constructor.
- Passing local_state via Mediator::FactoryImpl from ash_shell_init.cc.
- Removing #include "ash/shell.h" from //ash/quick_pair/repository.

dbf6f16c6c9167fa4a552d255f3dd2c059e4988c [ diff ] 
[views] Reuse pending buffers in ViewsAXManager and WidgetAXManager

ViewsAXManager::SendPendingUpdate() and
WidgetAXManager::SendPendingUpdate() move pending_events_ and
pending_data_updates_ into stack-local containers on each serialization
batch, freeing their backing storage at scope exit and churning
PartitionAlloc's quarantine on subsequent UI updates. Swap with
reusable member scratch buffers recycled via absl::Cleanup (and reset
on Disable()) to retain capacity across batches and pre-reserve the
serialized output vectors.

tree
8c21535a8818f1dae4bfe0cd1f0c3f8dccaf29b9
parent
e6ab1190f87064044d54c07ba4345967da7137e8 [ diff ] 
[Tab Groups] Focus dialog when other-window group open falls back

When opening a tab group whose state is GroupWindowState.IN_ANOTHER
from the Hub Tab Groups Pane, TabGroupRowMediator calls
TabGroupUiUtils.openTabGroup(...).

If the group is in an active other window, openTabGroup brings that
window to the foreground (returning ANOTHER_WINDOW). However,
if the other window is closed or cannot be brought to the foreground,
openTabGroup falls back to restoring and opening the group in the
current window's TabModel (returning CURRENT_WINDOW).

Previously, TabGroupRowMediator.openGroup() immediately returned after
calling TabGroupUiUtils.openTabGroup() for IN_ANOTHER groups without
checking whether it was opened in another window or in the current
window. As a result, when it fell back to opening locally in the
current window, the Hub Tab Groups Pane neither switched to the Tab
Switcher pane nor opened the tab group dialog, leaving the user on the
Tab Groups Pane with a silent no-op.

This CL updates TabGroupRowMediator.openGroup to check if
openTabGroup returned CURRENT_WINDOW; if so, it navigates
to the Tab Switcher pane and opens the Tab Group Dialog for the
restored group.

Bug: None

24558a8ff61521a7f7cd63ad60c74074bbf14dbb [ diff ] 
cros_healthd: Use USER_VISIBLE priority in GetTouchpadLibraryName

DataCollector::GetTouchpadLibraryName posted its blocking /dev/input/
query to ThreadPool with TaskPriority::BEST_EFFORT. When Chrome started
in Guest mode with --no-startup-window, BrowserMainLoop's
ScopedBestEffortExecutionFence blocked BEST_EFFORT tasks for 3 minutes
because no visible tab loaded, causing cros_healthd's FetchInputInfo
Mojo call (and tast.health.ProbeInputInfo) to time out after 60 seconds.

Raise the task priority to USER_VISIBLE while keeping MayBlock() on
ThreadPool so on-demand IPC requests complete during the startup fence.

Bug: b:580884490
Test: chromeos_unittests --gtest_filter=DataCollectorTest.*

108081fd776233bcdae5fef4d99fc287a9852e38 [ diff ] 
Reuse IsolationInfo::DoNotUseCreatePartialFromNak() in URLRequest

Delegate URLRequest::CreateIsolationInfoFromNetworkAnonymizationKey() to
IsolationInfo::DoNotUseCreatePartialFromNak() instead of duplicating its
implementation. (The `DoNotUse` prefix is regrettable, but using a
single helper is preferable over duplicating its implementation
elsewhere, and an incomplete version of it at that.)

This eliminates duplicate reconstruction logic and preserves the
NetworkIsolationPartition on both populated and empty
NetworkAnonymizationKeys.

This changes behavior: previously, if the NAK was empty,
CreateIsolationInfoFromNetworkAnonymizationKey would create an empty
IsolationInfo and ignore the NAK's network_isolation_partition field.
Now, the partition is preserved. (Added a regression test to verify.)

3f7e48f8456ca21e12e8c9d34ef361ce2364d86f [ diff ] 
Use DCHECK for IsValid check in AXPosition CompareTo

Revert the CHECK macro back to DCHECK for IsValid() on the current
position in AXPosition::CompareTo. When IsValid() evaluates to false
during AXPosition comparison in release builds, the CHECK causes a
crash. Reverting to DCHECK avoids crashes in release builds while
preserving assertion behavior in debug builds.

This fix was generated by the Crash Autofix pipeline for crash report
crash/9208c0ec08400000. Please see go/crash-autofix for more details.

parent
0fb5c8f033e5bfe56123c568413f170499034d7c [ diff ] 
Maintain active session state for side panel with context inputs

Treat contextual task sessions with unsubmitted composebox inputs (such as added tab or file contexts, pending uploads, or visual selections) as having results even while in zero state.

This tracks unsubmitted query status in ContextualTasksWebContentsUserData, updates it from ContextualTasksComposeboxHandler as contexts are modified, and considers it in ContextualTasksSidePanelCoordinator so side panel buttons remain active when the panel is closed.

73116f1f9e565d7e19041d8ead8e7e695455aa7f [ diff ] 
ash: Use ash::GetChannelName() instead of chrome/common/channel_info.h

On ChromeOS, chrome::GetChannelName() is a thin wrapper around
ash::GetChannelName() (see chrome/common/channel_info_chromeos.cc), and
WithExtendedStable has no effect there. chrome::GetVersionString() is
equivalent to
version_info::GetVersionStringWithModifier(ash::GetChannelName()).

Replace the remaining uses under chrome/browser/ash and
chrome/browser/ui/webui/ash with the //chromeos/ash/components/channel
API to drop the dependency on //chrome/common:channel_info. The feedback
unittests now compute the expected "CHROME VERSION" in the same way as
specialized_features::SendFeedback() does.
Also drop now-unused +chrome/common DEPS entries.

No behavior change.

Revert CHECK to DCHECK in GestureProvider::OnFling

Revert CHECK(scroll_event_sent_) in GestureProvider::OnFling back to a
DCHECK. Based on crash reports, scroll_event_sent_ can be false during a
fling event in release builds, triggering a fatal crash when CHECK is
used. Annotate the DCHECK with a TODO to convert it back to a CHECK once
we are confident it will not be triggered.

This fix was generated by the Crash Autofix pipeline for crash report
crash/82cfd9a8af800000. Please see go/crash-autofix for more details.

f8556531fff932edd1e400ef0ea8af78e4d5ac0c
parent
9bfc941003e01efe9532ad994f98bc806ea8160d [ diff ] 
Bookmark bar: C++ support for opening URL clicks on Android

Native-only half of "C++ Parity Navigation" for the shared bookmark bar
UI controller. Nothing calls it on Android yet; the Java wiring is the
next CL.

Shared controller (components/browser_apis/ui_controllers/bookmark_bar):
- PlatformAdaptersProvider::GetSupportedDispositions(): the controller
  resolves a click to a disposition the embedder supports
- javascript: bookmarks only open in the current tab.
- Desktop supports every disposition, so its behavior is unchanged.

Android adapters (chrome/browser/ui/bookmarks/controllers/android):
- AndroidBookmarkBarModelAdapter reads the plain BookmarkModel (like
  BookmarkBridge) instead of BookmarkMergedSurfaceService.
- AndroidBookmarkBarActionAdapter::OpenBookmark() opens bookmarks the
  way the Java BookmarkOpener does: Navigate() directly (sync for tabs,
  async for a new window, avoiding AndroidBrowserWindow::OpenURL's
  re-navigation when a javascript: URL has no handle), tab launch types
  FROM_BOOKMARK_BAR_BACKGROUND / FROM_LINK, bookmarklets only on a live
  non-native page, the bookmark ID on ChromeNavigationUIData, and
  BookmarkOpener's metrics with identical histogram parameters.

c2570f67fae60ca3d227f4f578e4cc3e656e9004
parent
fce632135f381fd4a0932dacb30fc46c0ae262d6 [ diff ] 
Android: Do not import build_utils in write_build_config.py

write_build_config.py runs for every java target, and importing
build_utils (which pulls in logging, subprocess, zipfile, etc.) is
slow. It was only used for WriteJson(), so move WriteJson() into
write_build_config.py (its only caller), and have it use binary I/O to
avoid encoding / decoding.

  small library:     53.4ms -> 41.5ms
  chrome_java:       159.7ms -> 148.1ms
  chrome_public_apk: 197.3ms -> 187.0ms
  largest test apk:  245.1ms -> 233.9ms

Measured with: hyperfine -N --warmup 3 --runs 30 (mean, vs. parent
commit) running write_build_config.py on an android x64 Debug build.

fce632135f381fd4a0932dacb30fc46c0ae262d6

Fri Nov 06 02:44:50 2026
tree
0ee4d52f5dd30a9848b8ea4ee007573ecc1b4c66
parent
2f86c6670d606ec2eb87fba9a3c88edfc07c6be1 [ diff ] 
ipcz: Skip redundant memset on freshly allocated shared memory

Newly allocated shared memory mappings in NodeLinkMemory are already
zero-initialized by the underlying OS/driver. Calling memset(0) in
BlockAllocator::InitializeRegion() forces immediate page faults across
the entire 128 KiB primary buffer per NodeLink pair even when only a few
blocks are used. On ChromeOS right after login, there are ~51 NodeLink
primary buffers (~6.4 MiB across Chrome and Mojo-connected system
daemons), so avoiding the upfront memset saves ~4 MiB of physical shmem
pages.

Document the zero-initialization requirement on
IpczDriver::AllocateSharedMemory(), add a multinode unit test verifying
it across drivers, and use BlockAllocator::InitializePrezeroedRegion()
when initializing newly allocated shared memory buffers.

e63b7c106e86a613a3ce75a1bcddcafababfccef [ diff ] 
[Reading mode PDFs] Keep text run bounds on one line after a link

Links end a text run at the link's last char, so the space and line
break after a link at the end of a line start the next run. That run's
bounds began at the space, on the previous line, and covered both lines.

Heuristics then read the run as continuing the line above. For example:

- A list item after a link merged into the paragraph or list item above.
- A heading after a link merged into the paragraph above instead of
  becoming a heading.
- A small caps line after a link was split, e.g. "G" and a superscript
  "ENERAL".

With heuristic enhancements on, skip leading whitespace that contains a
synthesized newline when computing run bounds. Move the newline check
from PDFiumEngine to PDFiumPage so the run code can use it.

Example screenshots:
before: https://screenshot.corp.google.com/2ol6mjs1gmop8
after: https://screenshot.corp.google.com/3jl80fiove2kg
before: https://screenshot.corp.google.com/70nir17ggbh6o
after: https://screenshot.corp.google.com/4f978al4qg4e8
before: https://screenshot.corp.google.com/143ev380vqln8
after: https://screenshot.corp.google.com/2gav053dfb930

[MemoryCoordinator] Remove SimulatePressureNotificationAsync()

All MemoryPressureListeners are now registered synchronously on the main
thread, so there is no pending registration to wait for. Remaining
callers now use SimulatePressureNotification(). The back/forward cache
tests keep running the loop afterwards to flush the eviction tasks
posted by the notification.

a172e44c7b1915d9825ac1e52675c36449f68019
parent
0ade1755f3df34f1d565b816b05defe96999980a [ diff ] 
net: Add MockNetworkChangeNotifier::SetConnectionCostAndNotifyObservers

MockNetworkChangeNotifier provides SetConnectionTypeAndNotifyObservers()
to atomically update the cached connection type and notify observers,
but only provides SetConnectionCost() for connection cost. Callers that
only invoke SetConnectionCost() do not trigger
OnConnectionCostChanged(), while callers that only invoke
NotifyObserversOfConnectionCostChangeForTests() leave
GetConnectionCost() returning the stale value inside observer callbacks.

Add SetConnectionCostAndNotifyObservers() to update the cached
connection cost, notify ConnectionCostObservers, and spin the message
loop, matching SetConnectionTypeAndNotifyObservers().

Design Doc: https://docs.google.com/document/d/1YX2axNYzC2mp0u6_qytjT_fts6MpdF_flud-ppj7e4A/edit?usp=drivesdk&resourcekey=0-A98JWXjsC2au1iQ7GTFi5g

Filter Plurals via featureFilters.misc in icu4x_datagen to match ICU4C

In ICU4C's `filtration.py`, `source/data/misc/plurals.txt`
(`plurals.res`) and `source/data/misc/pluralRanges.txt`
(`pluralRanges.res`) belong to the `misc` category rather than
`locales_tree` (`"misc".endswith("_tree")` is false), so `localeFilter`
never applies to them. Instead, they are filtered only by the
`featureFilters.misc` file-stem filter (`includelist` / `excludelist` of
`source/data/misc/*.txt` stems, where `flutter.json` and
`flutter_desktop.json` set `"misc": {"includelist": ["likelySubtags"]}`)
and have no `resourceFilters` rules across any of Chromium's 8 filter
files.

Update `Icu4cFilterCategory::Plurals` in `icu4x_datagen` to check
`featureFilters.misc` for the `"plurals"` stem and otherwise include all
CLDR plural locales (`ResolvedLocaleFilter::Full`, including `gv` and
`se`), and regenerate `plurals_{cardinal,ordinal,ranges}_v1.rs.data`.
Also add `tools/crates/icu4x_datagen/OWNERS`.

[remoting] Fix Windows DirectX capability check

On Windows hosts where DXGI output duplication is unsupported (for
example, hybrid-GPU laptops in docked or headless configurations),
EvaluateD3D() outputs "No-DirectX-Capturer" but still exits with
kSuccessExitCode. IsD3DAvailable() previously checked only the exit
code and ignored stdout, reporting DirectX capture as available even
when unsupported.

Additionally, DesktopEnvironmentFactory creates the interaction
strategy (which copies DesktopEnvironmentOptions by value) before
constructing BasicDesktopEnvironment, so updating
allow_directx_capturer in BasicDesktopEnvironment had no effect on
LegacyInteractionStrategy::CreateVideoCapturer().

- Update IsD3DAvailable() to inspect GetD3DCapabilities() via
  HasDirectXCapturerCapability() and log a warning when falling back
  to GDI.
- Move the Windows IsD3DAvailable() check from BasicDesktopEnvironment
  into LegacyInteractionStrategy's constructor so the capturer uses
  the updated option.
- Initialize exit_code in EvaluateCapability() to
  kInitializationFailed in case process launch fails.

Test: remoting_unittests --gtest_filter=EvaluateD3DTest.*

tree
12bfef33192b8c37cc5fae05ed6d640c3efa5733
parent
bd5af30d00fe69649a8325ad57424b55e9c54175 [ diff ] 
Linux: Don't fall back to the C locale when one LC_* locale is missing

setlocale(LC_ALL, "") fails as a whole, changing nothing, when any
single category names a locale that isn't installed, e.g.
LANG=en_US.UTF-8 with LC_TIME=fi_FI.UTF-8 but no fi_FI locale. This is
common on KDE, where Region & Language settings can select locales that
aren't generated, and in Flatpak, whose runtime only ships some
locales. The failure was ignored, so every process stayed in the "C"
locale, where mbstowcs() and friends fail on any non-ASCII byte. On
Linux, FilePath <-> UTF-16 conversions go through those functions, so
non-ASCII file names became empty strings: picked files showed up with
an empty name and 0 bytes, the renderer was killed with
RFH_CAN_ACCESS_FILES_OF_PAGE_STATE, and downloads named "ööö.txt" were
saved as "download".

Move the locale setup out of content_main.cc into
InitializeCLibraryLocale(). If setlocale(LC_ALL, "") fails, set each
category on its own so that the valid ones still apply, and if LC_CTYPE
itself can't be set, use "C.UTF-8" for it, which is no worse than "C"
for any file name encoding.

Fixed: 40251852

[webium] Fix RendererFreezer crash for top-chrome WebUI

RendererFreezer::OnRenderProcessLaunched() looks up the extension in
every new renderer through extensions::ProcessMap. The top-chrome
WebUI profile has no keyed services by design, so ProcessMap::Get()
returns null for it. On ChromeOS, the browser crashed as soon as
TopChromeWebUISharedRenderer launched the shared top-chrome WebUI
renderer.

Treat a missing ProcessMap as a renderer with no extension, as other
code that inspects every renderer does, so these renderers are frozen
on suspend like any other non-extension renderer.

Bug: 525236301
Test: unit_tests --gtest_filter=RendererFreezerTest*

41337ef7e825713e09b5c5796d31db25a975fcb0 [ diff ] 
[WebMCP] Add continuation tokens [2/N]; mojo, browser implementation

Explainer:
https://github.com/webmachinelearning/webmcp/blob/main/continuations-explainer.md

Implements single-hop WebMCP continuation tokens in Blink and the
browser process. A tool callback calls options.invocation.requestToken()
to mint a single-use token. A same-origin document in the same tab can
then redeem it with document.modelContext.resumeTool(token, callback),
including after a cross-document navigation. The resumed callback's
result becomes the result of the original tool call.

Mojo:
- Adds RequestContinuationToken() and RedeemContinuationToken() to
  blink.mojom.ModelContextHost, and ResumeScriptTool() to
  blink.mojom.ModelContext.
- Adds blink.mojom.ContinuationToken, mapped to blink::ContinuationToken
  (a base::StrongAlias of base::Uuid). Tokens are RFC 4122 v4 UUIDs, and
  the mojo traits reject anything else.

Browser (content/browser/renderer_host/):
- ModelContextContinuationManager (per WebContents) tracks each
  ContinuedToolExecution so tokens survive navigations of the primary
  main frame and same-origin subframe handoffs.
- A token can be redeemed as soon as it is minted. From that point the
  continuation determines the outcome of the original tool call, however
  the requesting callback settles (resolves, rejects, or its document
  unloads first).
- Redemption replies to RedeemContinuationToken() before sending
  ResumeScriptTool() on the same ordered channel, so the resumeTool()
  Promise and its reactions settle before the resumed callback runs.
- Cancelling the original tool call aborts an executing resumed step but
  leaves an unredeemed token redeemable until it expires
  (continuation_timeout, 30s by default).
- The continuation fails if the token expires, the resumed step rejects,
  or the redeeming document unloads before the resumed step completes.
- Kills the renderer (RFH_INVALID_TOOL_CONTINUATION) on invalid
  requests, e.g. when continuations are disabled or a document requests
  a token for a tool call it isn't running.

Blink:
- ModelContext implements requestToken(), resumeTool(), and resumed
  callback execution. Calling requestToken() from a resumed step rejects
  with InvalidStateError; chained continuations are not yet supported.
- Adds WTF HashTraits for base::Uuid and base::StrongAlias.

Gated by the WebMCPContinuations runtime feature (experimental), with a
default-enabled base::Feature of the same name as a browser kill switch.

Bug: 555180336

[Android] Fix PathUtilsTest on multi-user devices

PathUtilsTest.TestGetDataDirectory and
PathUtilsTest.TestGetCacheDirectory previously only accepted
/data/data/... or /data/user/0/... paths. On multi-user Android devices
where tests run as a secondary user (for example user 10 under headless
system user mode), Android returns /data/user/<user_id>/... and both
tests fail.

Match /data/(data|user/\d+)/... with ::testing::MatchesRegex and verify
that the returned directory exists.

TAG=agy
CONV=530cea9c-5099-4041-976a-927b32a5bf2b

siso: add zip_devtools_frontend.py to denylist

zip_devtools_frontend.py dynamically reads files listed in JSON metadata
files (expected_grd_files.json and remote_files.json generated via GN
data_keys) without declaring them as action inputs (relying on depfiles
instead), which causes FileNotFoundError in RBE sandboxes on clean

[History] Gate day-card collapse behind flag and remove domain collapse

Follow up to https://crrev.com/c/8681441 to align with History product
and UX plans:
- Revert the same-day website domain group collapse/expand UI and state
  tracking so website-level grouping remains reserved for the upcoming
  CAL feature.
- Gate day-card collapse/expand in chrome://history behind a
  disabled-by-default base::Feature flag
  (kBrowsingHistoryCollapsibleDayCards) while UX alignment is in
  progress.

e0c4b62dee132ce1470f4386daa2f1bd84673b2e [ diff ] 
[remoting] Add Windows host audit OS probe and SDDL validators

Adds the foundational OS probing interface and security descriptor / ACL
validators for auditing Chrome Remote Desktop Windows MSI and EXE host
installations (1/3):

- PRESUBMIT.py to run Python unit tests in
  //remoting/host/installer/win/setup
- SystemProbe and WindowsSystemProbe (Win32 ctypes/WOW64 registry,
  filesystem reparse/SDDL, SCM service, relative binary SD bounds
  validation via RtlValidRelativeSecurityDescriptor, and MSI product
  enumeration)
- validate_sddl and validate_com_permissions_sddl to verify owner, DACL
  protection, safe non-admin read/execute bitmasks across file/registry/
  service objects, AppContainer Read+Execute inheritance, and COM
  Launch/AccessPermission SDDLs
- FakeSystemProbe and unit tests in audit_host_install_unittest.py

Bug: 551615326, 581913324

Add WebUI extensions request access button

To support ExtensionsMenuAccessControl, show an extension request access
button when extensions are requesting access to the current site. This
button contains text and appears as the left half of the extensions menu
button (the puzzle piece). The shared button is drawn using two
toolbar-chip-buttons with flat edges to make them appear as one button.
When clicked, the access is granted and the button shows a confirmation
for a few seconds before disappearing.

8932329a5168f12f687607518f70959abb69260f [ diff ] 
Open external URLs in the last active tabbed browser

Since crrev.com/c/7279203, GetExistingBrowserForOpenBehavior() starts
from the last active browser of any type and only looks for a tabbed
browser on the current workspace when that workspace is known. On Linux
on Wayland the current workspace is always unknown (and macOS has no
workspace lookup), so when an app window such as an installed PWA was
the last active window, that window was returned, OpenTabsInBrowser()
rejected it as not tabbed, and the URL opened in a new window instead
of as a tab in the existing one.

Fall back to the most recently active tabbed browser of the profile
instead, as chrome::FindTabbedBrowser() did before. The workspace
preferences on Windows, ChromeOS and Linux are unchanged.

Fixed: 581932209

updater: log the exit code of processes the integration tests run

updater::test::Run() now logs the exit code of a process that exits.
When the process does not exit within the timeout, the assertion names
the process ID and the timeout. It used to log the thread's last error
code, which a timeout does not set, so the log showed an unrelated
error such as "Cannot create a file when that file already exists".

Also document when Run() waits, and that a timeout leaves the exit
code unchanged.

[Extensions] Apply $i18n{...} replacements for disk-loaded resources

When a component extension with registered template replacements is
served from the grit resource bundle (.pak), ResourceBundleFileLoader
applies GetTemplateReplacementsForExtension() via
ui::ReplaceTemplateExpressions() and
ui::ReplaceTemplateExpressionsInJS().

However, when the same extension is loaded from disk (e.g., staged via
Component Updater in ComponentLoader::MaybeLoadStagedExtension),
ExtensionURLLoader bypasses GetTemplateReplacementsForExtension() and
serves the raw files directly, leaving $i18n{...} placeholders
unexpanded.

This change:

- Shares template replacement helpers in extensions::url_request_util
  between ResourceBundleFileLoader (.pak loads) and ExtensionURLLoader
  (disk loads).

- Adds ui::TryReplaceTemplateExpressions() so disk-loaded resources
  fail gracefully (serving the file unchanged) instead of crashing the
  browser process with a CHECK if an out-of-band extension bundle
  references an $i18n{...} key not yet present in the browser binary's
  GetLoadTimeData(), while preserving the CHECK for .pak and WebUI
  loads.

- Adds an explicit ExtensionConfigProvider::ShouldReplaceI18nTemplates()
  opt-in (defaulting to false) so extensions that only use loadTimeData
  via /strings.m.js and I18nMixin do not incur template replacement
  overhead on .pak or disk loads.

Show send spinner while holding a pending query

Since crrev.com/c/8501220, a query can be held until its attachments
finish uploading, e.g. a Co-browse follow-up sent with the lazily
uploaded auto-added tab. While the query was held, nothing showed the
wait: the typed text is cleared and the send button only looks disabled.

Show an activity indicator in the send button while the query is held.
Keeping the typed text is tracked in crbug.com/582333512.

- Before/After (follow-up waiting on the tab upload): https://screenshot.googleplex.com/VpmPDP9GDtm5aea
